TEERTHANKER MAHAVEER UNIVERSITY
(Established under Govt. of U. P . Act No. 30, 2008)
Delhi Road, Moradabad (U.P)
jointregistrar@tmu.ac.in
Phone No.: 0591-2476813 Date: 08-January-2026
Ref. No : TMU/R.O./2025-26/Adm/61 Prepared by: Associate Dean R&D
Policy No : 5.1 Issued by: Joint Registrar
Total Pages : 19 Approved by: Vice Chancellor
TEERTHANKER MAHA VEER UNIVERSITY (TMU) IPR POLICY
i. These guidelines shall be called the Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) IPR Policy.
ii. These guidelines shall apply to all faculty members, staff, research fellows, Ph.D. scholars,
students, and other stakeholders of Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU).
iii. These policy guidelines shall come into force with effect from the date of approval.
I. PREAMBLE
Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) is committed to fostering a culture of innovation,
research excellence, and knowledge dissemination. A core mission of TMU is to nurture human
intellectual potential to its fullest by promoting original thinking, creativity, and innovation
among its faculty, students, and researchers. Recognizing the diverse and dynamic nature of
research and development (R&D) across the University, TMU acknowledges the importance
of protecting and managing intellectual assets arising from these activities. The University
community regularly engages in the creation of valuable intellectual property (IP) in the form
of patents, copyrights, trademarks, and industrial designs.
To maximize the societal and economic impact of its innovations, TMU actively supports the
protection and commercialization of intellectual property. The University encourages the
licensing of its IP to industries, startups, and organizations capable of bringing these
innovations to market, thereby contributing to national development and generating revenue
for reinvestment into research. Additionally, TMU strongly encourages its students,
researchers, and faculty members to establish startups leveraging University-generated IP,
fostering an entrepreneurial ecosystem within the campus. In line with this vision, TMU has
established the Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell under the governance of the Research
and Development Center (RDC). The RDC evaluates the novelty, feasibility, and utility of
inventions and facilitates their protection through formal IPR filings. Importantly, TMU
promotes a fair and rewarding incentive system for inventors. In cases where University-owned
intellectual property is commercialized, the net revenue generated shall be distributed as
follows: 60% to the inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s), and the remaining 40% to the University.
This revenue-sharing model is designed to incentivize innovation while supporting academic
units and institutional growth. This model aims to recognize the contribution of innovators
Page 2 of 19
while ensuring sustainable support for institutional research infrastructure. This Intellectual
Property Policy, developed by the RDC, provides a structured framework for the creation,
protection, ownership, and commercialization of intellectual property developed at TMU. It
aims to safeguard the interests of all stakeholders while advancing the University’s
commitment to innovation and societal benefit.
II. PURPOSE AND OBJECTIVE
The primary objectives of Teerthanker Mahaveer University’s (TMU) Intellectual Property
Rights (IPR) Policy are to:
 Foster a culture of innovation and creativity among faculty, researchers, and students,
leading to the development of novel ideas, technologies, and knowledge assets.
 Facilitate the identification, protection, and commercialization of intellectual property
generated within the University through a structured mechanism administered by the
Research and Development Center (RDC).
 Support inventors in evaluating the patentability, feasibility, and commercial potential of
their research outcomes, with assistance for both national and international IP filings.
 Promote the effective transfer of University-generated intellectual assets to industry,
startups, and other commercial entities, thereby contributing to societal advancement and
economic growth.
 Encourage and support faculty members, researchers, and students in launching startups
based on University-owned or jointly developed intellectual property, as part of the
University's commitment to nurturing entrepreneurship and innovation-driven enterprises.
 Provide capacity-building through education and training on IPR awareness, filing
procedures, commercialization strategies, and international IP systems to empower the
University community.
 Establish a transparent and equitable administrative framework for the ownership,
management, licensing, and revenue sharing of IP developed at TMU.
 Reward successful commercialization of University IP, with 60% to the
inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s), and the remaining 40% to the University for reinvestment
into research, innovation promotion, and IPR facilitation activities.
III. DEFINITIONS
The terms used in this policy shall have the meanings as defined below, unless the context
requires otherwise:
Page 3 of 19
a) Intellectual Property (IP)
Intellectual Property refers to any form of knowledge, innovation, or creative expression
resulting from intellectual effort that is eligible for legal protection. The following
categories are typically recognized:
i. Patents: Scientific or technological inventions, including new processes, products,
compositions, biological materials, or software, that satisfy the criteria of novelty,
inventive step, and industrial applicability as defined under relevant patent laws.
ii. Industrial Designs: The aesthetic or ornamental features of an article—such as shape,
configuration, pattern, or color that enhance its visual appeal and are capable of being
protected under applicable design laws.
iii. Copyrights: Original literary, artistic, and creative works such as books, teaching
materials, research papers, software code, musical compositions, and multimedia
content, which are protected under copyright legislation.
iv. Trademarks: Unique signs, symbols, names, or logos that distinguish the goods or
services of one entity from those of others in the marketplace, and are protected under
trademark law.
v. Geographical Indications (GIs): Identifiers used on products that originate from a
specific geographical area and possess qualities, reputation, or characteristics that are
inherently linked to that location.
vi. Integrated Circuit Layout Designs: The three-dimensional configuration of electronic
circuits used in microchips and semiconductor devices, which are eligible for protection
under relevant laws governing layout-designs of integrated circuits.
vii. Plant Varieties: New, distinct, uniform, and stable plant varieties developed through
breeding or discovery, including extant and farmers’ varieties, which are eligible for
protection under the Protection of Plant Varieties and Farmers’ Rights Act, 2001, as
amended from time to time.
b) University
Refers to Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) , including all its departments, research
centers and constituent colleges.
c) Vice Chancellor
The Vice Chancellor of Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU), serving as the final authority
in all matters pertaining to University policy, including decisions related to intellectual property
and its governance.
Page 4 of 19
d) Governing Authority
All matters related to Intellectual Property Rights (IPRs) at Teerthanker Mahaveer University
(TMU) shall be assessed, governed, and approved by the Intellectual Property Rights (IPR)
Cell under the governance of the Research and Development center (RDC). The RDC serves
as the nodal body responsible for the evaluation, documentation, protection, and management
of IP generated within the University ecosystem.
e) Third Party
For the purpose of this policy, a third party refers to any individual, organization, or legal entity
that is not a student, employee, faculty member, or officially affiliated member of Teerthanker
Mahaveer University (TMU). This includes, but is not limited to, private companies, public
sector undertakings, other academic or research institutions, start-ups, incubators, accelerators,
technology transfer offices (TTOs), legal or IPR firms, patent/trademark agents, and
consultants.
Third parties may engage with the University for the purpose of licensing, commercialization,
collaborative research, legal representation, or any other activities involving intellectual
property developed at or associated with TMU.
f) IPR Intake Documentation
To ensure uniformity, proper evaluation, and timely processing of intellectual property filings,
the IPR Cell shall implement a standardized IPR intake form system. This system will serve as
the formal entry point for all IP-related submissions and shall include the following:
 Invention Disclosure Form (IDF) for patents: To document technical details, novelty, prior
art, and inventor contribution.
 Copyright Intake Form : To capture authorship, originality, category of work, and any
institutional involvement.
 Design Intake Form : To document industrial design features, originality, visual aspects,
and use of the TMU resources.
 Trademark Intake Form : For brand names, logos, taglines, or other identifiers developed
under TMU-supported initiatives.
 Integrated Circuit Layout Intake Form: To document the design, function, originality, and
layout pattern of integrated circuits developed using TMU resources.
All individuals (faculty, researchers, students, staff) seeking IP protection must submit the
appropriate intake form to the IPR Cell. These forms will serve as the basis for evaluation by
Page 5 of 19
the IPR Cell, and no filing shall proceed without the successful submission and approval of the
corresponding intake documentation.
g) Inventor/Author/Creator
Any employee of Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU), including those on probation,
those employed either by the University or through projects as well as research workers,
research scholars, or students who are responsible for the creation of Intellectual Property using
the facilities of the University.
h) Significant Use of University Resources
Substantial utilization of university physical, financial, human, or intellectual resources in the
creation or development of intellectual property. This includes extended use of labs, specialized
equipment/software, direct institutional funding/support, and considerable involvement of
university personnel. Incidental use (like library access) does not typically constitute
significant use.
i) Course Material
Any content, resources, or educational aids developed for instruction, including lecture notes,
slides, syllabi, curricula, audio/video content, educational software, digital modules, and
assessment tools.
j) Patentee
The individual or legal entity entered in the Register of Patents as the lawful proprietor or
assignee of a granted patent under the Indian Patents Act, 1970. In the context of Teerthanker
Mahaveer University (TMU), the University shall ordinarily be the patentee or applicant for
patents filed with its support, with the inventor(s) duly acknowledged as inventors. A
Certificate of Inventorship may be issued by the Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell upon
request by the inventors, subject to verification of their contribution.
k) Industrial Design Applicant/Owner
For industrial designs filed under the Designs Act, 2000, the legal applicant is the individual
or entity listed in the official Register of Designs. In the case of designs created using the
resources/support of Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU), TMU shall be the applicant,
and the creator of the design shall be duly acknowledged as a co-applicant in the filing.
Page 6 of 19
l) Copyright Ownership
Under the provisions of the Copyright Act, 1957, all copyrights—including those for literary,
artistic, dramatic, software, educational materials, or designs—shall be owned by Teerthanker
Mahaveer University (TMU) when created as part of any academic program of TMU or
pursuant to a written agreement with TMU that provides for the transfer of copyright or
ownership to the university. More specifically:
a) TMU shall own the copyright of all teaching materials created by TMU and non-TMU
personnel for external agencies, institutions, and industries under its continuing education,
online or distance education programs. However, the authors shall retain the right to use
such materials for their own teaching and research activities.
b) TMU shall not claim ownership of copyrights for books and scientific articles authored
independently by TMU personnel. However, if books or reports are created as an outcome
of any conference, seminar, Faculty Development Program (FDP), conclave, workshop,
etc., using funds specifically provided by TMU for that purpose, the copyright shall belong
to TMU.
c) If any employee wishes to retain sole ownership of the copyright for works such as literary,
artistic, dramatic, software, educational materials, or designs, they must obtain a No
Objection Certificate (NOC) from the university prior to filing the copyright application.
d) The ownership rights of any dissertation or thesis shall belong to TMU.
m) Trademark Ownership
Under the Trade Marks Act, 1999, a trademark is a distinctive sign, symbol, name, logo, design,
or expression that identifies and distinguishes the products or services of a particular source.
At Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU), any trademark or brand name developed as part
of University-funded or University-supported initiatives, including academic or research
programs, incubation projects, student competitions, or through the use of TMU’s
infrastructure, resources, or guidance, shall be filed and owned in the name of TMU as the
applicant/legal proprietor. The creator(s), innovator(s), or contributor(s) such as faculty,
students, or staff or any former employees shall be duly acknowledged in internal records and
may be recognized as co-developers or contributors for academic or institutional purposes.
However, this does not confer legal ownership of the trademark unless a specific agreement
stating otherwise is executed in writing.
n) Geographical Indications (GIs)
Under the applicable laws, a Geographical Indication (GI) is an identifier used on products that
originate from a specific geographical location and possess distinct qualities, reputation, or
Page 7 of 19
characteristics inherently linked to that region. At Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU), if
a GI application is initiated as part of a University-supported project, research initiative, or
investigation involving the use of TMU’s infrastructure, resources, or academic engagement,
the Principal Investigator (PI) and research contributors shall be acknowledged as the
investigators. The GI application shall typically be filed in the name of the concerned trust,
community, or producer group as required under GI law. However, the PI’s address in the
application shall reflect their affiliation with TMU, ensuring institutional visibility and
recognition.
o) Integrated Circuit Layout Designs
Integrated Circuit Layout Designs refer to the three-dimensional configurations of electronic
circuits used in microchips and semiconductor devices and are protected under the
Semiconductor Integrated Circuits Layout-Design Act, 2000. At Teerthanker Mahaveer
University (TMU), any integrated circuit layout design developed using University funding,
facilities, or during the course of employment or academic engagement shall be owned by
TMU, and the layout design shall be filed in the name of TMU as the legal proprietor. The
contributors, including faculty, students, or staff involved in the development, shall be
acknowledged as inventors or designers in the University’s records, and recognized
appropriately for their contribution.
p) Ownership Rights Over Plant Varieties
Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) recognizes and upholds the importance of protecting
innovations in the field of plant breeding. The University adheres to the provisions of the
Protection of Plant Varieties and Farmers’ Rights Act, 2001, as amended from time to time.
TMU shall facilitate the protection of eligible Plant Varieties developed by its faculty,
researchers, or students under this Act, subject to the fulfillment of statutory requirements and
compliance with institutional procedures. The ownership rights over any Plant Variety
developed using the resources of the University shall vest with TMU. However, if it is
determined that a Plant Variety was developed independently by an individual or group of
individuals—entirely on their own time, unrelated to their assigned responsibilities at TMU,
and without the use of any institutional resources—the ownership rights shall vest with the
individual(s) concerned.
q) Compliance with The Biological Diversity Act, 2002
All research activities, academic projects, and intellectual property applications undertaken
under the aegis of Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) that utilize, involve, or are derived
Page 8 of 19
from India’s biological resources and/or associated traditional knowledge must fully comply
with the provisions of The Biological Diversity Act, 2002. This includes, but is not limited to,
research leading to intellectual property (IP) such as patents, copyrights, trademarks, or any
other IP rights involving biological material or knowledge of Indian origin.
Before initiating any IP application related to such biological resources or traditional
knowledge, the involved personnel including faculty members, staff, research fellows, Ph.D.
scholars, students, and other stakeholders are required to obtain prior approval or necessary
permissions from the National Biodiversity Authority (NBA), as mandated under the Act. The
University shall provide guidance and support in facilitating the approval process. TMU will
not be liable for any IP application or activity that fails to adhere to these statutory obligations.
r) Student-Created Intellectual Property (IP)
Students normally own the intellectual property (IP) they develop as part of coursework or
independent academic activities, provided they use only standard University resources (such
as general computer labs, libraries, and standard software). For works involving collaborative
research with faculty or where there is substantial use of university resources or funding (for
example, lab work, funded research, or sponsored projects), the University may claim
ownership or joint ownership of the resulting IP. Often, students must assign IP rights to the
University as a condition of participation or receipt of funding for such projects. If a student is
working as an intern or research fellow in the University or paid research assistant, any IP
developed within the scope of those job duties or as a result of funded research is typically
owned by the University, not the student. This includes, but is not limited to, inventions,
creative works, software, patents, designs, trademarks, and other intellectual property
generated as part of employment or funded assignments.
s) Ownership of Independently Developed Intellectual Property
Works created without significant university support meaning those developed, owned solely
by their creators (whether faculty, staff, or students). Standard resources, such as general access
to libraries, ordinary computer labs, or basic administrative support, do not constitute
significant support. Therefore, if intellectual property (IP) including but not without substantial
use of university resources, funding, or personnel are typically limited to inventions, designs,
trademarks, software, or copyrightable works is generated independently and outside formal
university projects, the individual creator usually retains all ownership rights, unless otherwise
specified by written agreements or sponsorship terms. This approach aligns with best practices
and upholds academic freedom and individual incentive to innovate.
Page 9 of 19
t) Revenue
Revenue refers to any financial or in-kind returns received by the University from the licensing,
assignment, or commercialization of its intellectual property. This includes, but is not limited
to, royalty payments, milestone payments, equity shares, or lump sum considerations received
under legally binding agreements. Such revenue shall be managed in accordance with the
University’s policies on IP ownership, benefit sharing, and reinvestment in research and
innovation.
IV. SCOPE OF THE POLICY
1. The Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell, under the governance of the Research and
Development Centre (RDC), shall facilitate and support faculty, researchers, and students
in the identification, protection, and commercialization of all forms of intellectual property
including inventions, designs, copyrights, trademarks, integrated circuit layouts, software,
plant varieties, and artistic or creative works. The rules and regulations outlined in this
policy document shall apply to all IP-related activities undertaken by the University. These
rules and regulations shall come into effect from the date of notification.
2. Faculty members and students who intend to file any IPR (e.g., patent, copyright,
trademark, design, plant varieties, integrated circuit layouts) shall receive necessary advice,
assistance, and legal guidance from the Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell.
3. A duly filled IPR Intake Form, specific to the type of Intellectual Property (as provided by
the IPR Cell), must be submitted. The form should include complete details of all
inventors/authors/creators, along with a comprehensive description of the IPR sought to be
protected. This form shall be submitted to the IPR Cell for evaluation and consideration for
filing.
4. All disclosures related to Intellectual Property Rights (IPRs) and associated documents
shall be treated with strict confidentiality by the IPR Cell and all relevant authorities
involved in the evaluation and protection process.
5. All costs associated with the filing, prosecution, and maintenance (including renewal fees)
of IPRs shall be borne by Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU), subject to the approval
of the competent authority and in accordance with the University’s budgetary provisions
and IP strategy.
6. If the Research and Development center (RDC) identifies significant potential in the
innovation disclosed by the faculty, a decision may be made to pursue international
protection. For patents, this may include filing a Patent Cooperation Treaty (PCT)
application, and for trademarks, filing under the Madrid Protocol or directly in foreign
Page 10 of 19
jurisdictions, as applicable. Such filings aim to secure legal protection in multiple countries,
subject to feasibility, commercial viability, and strategic interest.
7. The RDC, in coordination with the IPR Cell, shall liaise with the relevant IP offices, legal
firms, registered patent/trademark agents, international filing agencies, and the concerned
inventors/authors/creators to ensure the effective prosecution, management, and protection
of intellectual property both domestically and internationally.
8. Inventors, authors, or creators shall fully cooperate with the IPR Cell by promptly providing
all necessary information and documents, in order to avoid delays or penalties during the
prosecution of intellectual property.
9. Any work resulting from research, innovation, or academic activities conducted at
Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) shall be filed in the name of Teerthanker
Mahaveer University (TMU) as the applicant and legal owner of the intellectual property,
unless otherwise decided by the RDC based on specific circumstances.
10. The IPR Cell for Intellectual Property Facilitation has to be constituted to advise, support,
and guide the IPR related matters like IP filings, strategy, protection, and
commercialization. The IPR Cell would take the advice and approvals on policy and
strategic matters from University Research Advisory Committee (URAC). The IPR Cell
shall include the following members:
1. Dean (R&D)/ Associate Dean (R&D), Chairperson
2. Principal of the College Concerned, Member
3. Associate Dean (Ph.D Cell), Member
4. Coordinator (R&D), Member
5. IP Manager, Member Secretary
V. TIMELINES AND TURNAROUND
To ensure transparency and efficiency in the IP management process, the following standard
turnaround times shall be observed:
 The IPR Cell shall evaluate each IPR intake form within 10 days from the date of
submission. If the disclosed subject matter is found to meet the criteria for intellectual
property protection, the IPR Cell will initiate the filing process in consultation with the
RDC. In case TMU decides to relinquish its claim over ownership, the decision must be
duly documented and approved by the competent authority. However, in such case, the
TMU IPR Cell shall not provide any assistance with IP protection.
 The IP filing process (drafting and submission) must be initiated within 10 working days
from the date of receipt of the complete intake form.
Page 11 of 19
 For collaborative IP filings or licensing agreements, a draft proposal shall be provided to
the concerned parties within 20 working days from the date of the agreement.
 Feedback or a decision on commercialization proposals (e.g., licensing or startup-based
utilization) shall be communicated within 20 working days from the date of receipt.
These timelines may be extended only under exceptional circumstances, subject to a written
justification by the concerned inventor(s), creator(s), or author(s), and with the approval of the
Vice Chancellor.
VI. TERMS AND CONDITIONS IF IP IS NOT FILED BY TMU BUT FILED BY THIRD
PARTY
If an employee approaches the university's IPR cell to file intellectual property (IP) for their
own work, and the IPR cell believes that the IP is not worth filing, the university may issue a
No Objection Certificate (NOC) to the inventor, stating that it has no objection if they choose
to file the IP independently.
VII. IP LICENSING AND AGREEMENTS
Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) recognizes the legitimate commercial needs and the
importance of protecting intellectual property (IP), particularly for breakthrough technologies.
TMU strives to balance these needs with its core academic mission of disseminating research
and facilitating the practical application of innovations. IP licensing at TMU is overseen by the
Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell in coordination with the Research and Development
Center (RDC), which manages the evaluation, marketing, negotiations, and licensing of all
University-owned IP.
In certain cases, Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) may engage third-party agencies
such as technology transfer offices (TTOs), intellectual property (IP) law firms,
commercialization consultants, or industry liaison firms to facilitate the licensing, marketing,
or transfer of developed technologies. These agencies assist in identifying potential licensees
or investors, drafting and negotiating licensing agreements, assessing market potential, and
ensuring legal compliance. Such engagements shall be carried out under mutually agreed terms
and in accordance with the provisions of the TMU IPR Policy.
i. Licensing Types
The type of license granted will depend on the nature and commercial potential of the invention
or innovation. As an educational institution committed to national development, Teerthanker
Page 12 of 19
Mahaveer University (TMU) generally promotes non-exclusive licensing to enable wider
adoption and deployment of University-generated technologies.
a. Non-exclusive License: A non-exclusive license allows multiple licensees to use the same
intellectual property (IP) simultaneously. TMU retains the right to license the same IP to other
parties, and to use it for its own academic or research purposes.
However, in certain cases—such as when substantial investment is required from the licensee,
or when the IP serves as a platform technology TMU may consider granting application-
specific, region-specific, or fully exclusive licenses, depending on the strategic and commercial
requirements.
b. Exclusive License: An exclusive license grants the licensee sole rights to use the IP within a
defined field or territory. TMU agrees not to license the same IP to any other party and may
also restrict its own use, subject to terms of the agreement.
All licensing decisions will be preceded by a thorough due diligence process, which includes
evaluation of the licensee’s business plan, intended use of the IP, investment strategy, market
potential, and other relevant factors.
Licenses shall be issued only to registered companies (not individuals) and may be restricted
to the specific version of the IP being licensed—excluding any future updates, modifications,
or enhancements, unless otherwise agreed upon.
All licensing arrangements will be subject to periodic review by the Intellectual Property Rights
(IPR) Cell in coordination with the Research and Development Center (RDC). Based on the
outcomes of these reviews—especially in terms of usage status and societal or commercial
impact—TMU reserves the right to modify, extend, or terminate the license.
ii. License Preferences and Exemptions
If both internal inventors and external organizations express interest in licensing the same
TMU-owned IP, preference may be given to the internal inventors, depending on the nature of
the technology and other relevant factors.
Regardless of license terms, TMU retains perpetual institute-wide rights for academic and
research use, including rights to publish, reuse technical data, and utilize methods or products
derived from licensed IP for educational purposes.
Page 13 of 19
In the case of inventions developed by faculty, students, research scholars, or staff during
sabbatical, deputation, internships, or external visits, TMU reserves rights for academic usage
under research exemption and fair use policies. All stakeholders are encouraged to disclose
such inventions through the University's IPR intake form.
iii. Technology License / Transfer Options
TMU recognizes the critical role of inventors in successful commercialization. The University
may license IP through direct agreements with third parties, via the University incubation
center, or through authorized licensing agents. Preference will be given to licensing over
outright assignment of IP rights. TMU retains march-in rights for assigned IP to ensure proper
utilization.
a) Technology Licensing
Technology licensing will follow the prevailing TMU revenue-sharing model, under which
revenues shall be shared in a 60:40 ratios, as follows:
 60% to the inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s)
 40% to the University for reinvestment into research, innovation promotion, and IPR
facilitation activities
 In cases involving multiple inventors, revenue will be shared equally unless otherwise
agreed in writing by the inventors.
This model shall remain in effect for the duration of the commercialization term, unless
renegotiated or terminated by mutual agreement. Even if the inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s)
leave the University, the model shall continue. The share of the inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s)
will be declared annually, and disbursement shall be made to them or their legal heirs,
regardless of whether they are associated with TMU at the time of disbursement. The
Distribution of IP Earnings Agreement may be revised at any time by mutual consent. If any
student has made a significant contribution to the development of the intellectual property, they
shall be duly credited and included in the Income Distribution Form.
Key features include:
 Preference for non-exclusive licensing, unless exclusivity is warranted
 Exclusive licenses subject to periodic review
Page 14 of 19
b) Licensing Through entities/ organizations
In certain cases, TMU may engage third-party entities/ organizations approved by RDC to
facilitate technology transfer/licensing. These agreements will typically involve non-exclusive
terms, with periodic reviews to assess the licensee’s performance.
If TMU is unable to commercialize the intellectual property (IP) within a reasonable timeframe,
the inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s) may formally request a non-exclusive license to
independently pursue commercialization. In such cases, TMU shall not relinquish ownership
of the IP; however, it may grant the inventors a time-bound, non-exclusive license to
commercialize the IP under mutually agreed terms. The duration and scope of the license shall
be determined through mutual agreement between the inventors and the Research Development
Center (RDC), in consultation with the Vice Chancellor. This license will be subject to periodic
review, and TMU reserves the right to extend, modify, or terminate the license based on
performance, impact, and compliance with the agreed terms.
VIII. IPR OWNERSHIP OF TECHNOLOGIES DEVELOPED AT UNIVERSITY
The University IPR Cell shall provide comprehensive support for prior art searches, filing of
IPR applications, and facilitation of office action responses, hearings, and the grant of IP rights.
If University resources such as research infrastructure, manpower, or funding are used, or if
the IPR is developed as part of academic activities, the IP shall be deemed the absolute property
of TMU.
The Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell in coordination with the Research and Development
Center (RDC) shall oversee the licensing of such IP in consultation with the inventor(s).
Licensing arrangements may involve:
 Upfront or one-time technology transfer fees
 Royalty payments as a percentage of product sale price or per product sold
 Equity stake in the licensing company
As TMU cannot legally hold equity, the Teerthanker Mahaveer Innovation Foundation (TMIF),
a Section 8 company (implementation agency) for incubation and startup schemes, may be
designated to hold equity stakes in startups on behalf of the University, subject to applicable
legal and financial governance.
If the inventor(s)/creator(s)/author(s), choose to establish a start-up to commercialize their own
intellectual property, the royalty payable to the University shall not exceed 4% of the sale price
or net sales. In such cases, the equity stake to be held by the University (or its designated entity)
may range between 2% to 5%.
Page 15 of 19
For software products, the revenue-sharing terms shall be mutually agreed upon between the
start-up and the University, based on the nature and scope of commercialization.
In other cases, where TMU faculty members or students (who are not the original inventors or
creators) express interest in establishing a start-up or commercializing existing University-
owned IP, the royalty payable shall not exceed 6% of the sale price or net sales (turnover). The
equity stake in such cases may range between 3% to 5%.
For University-supported IP, the IPR Cell and the Incubation Center shall facilitate its
protection and commercialization. IP generated without the use of University resources shall
remain entirely under the control of the inventors, including decisions related to its filing,
unless otherwise decided by the Research and Development Center (RDC) with the advice of
the Vice Chancellor.
IX. JOINT IP OWNERSHIP AND EXTERNAL COLLABORATIONS
a) Collaborative IP Ownership
In cases where intellectual property is developed through collaborations with external
organizations, such as academic institutions, research organizations, industry partners, or
funding agencies, the ownership of the resulting IP shall be governed by the terms outlined in
the Memorandum of Understanding (MoU), contract, or agreement executed between TMU
and the external party.
b) MoU and Contractual Obligations
All collaborative research and development engagements must be formalized through a legally
binding MoU or agreement that clearly outlines:
 Ownership of foreground and background IP
 Revenue-sharing arrangements
 Licensing rights (exclusive/non-exclusive)
 Responsibilities for IP filing and maintenance costs
 Dispute resolution mechanisms
 Confidentiality obligations and publication rights
c) Default Position
In the absence of a specific agreement, IP developed jointly using TMU’s resources or
infrastructure shall be co-owned by TMU and the collaborating entity , with TMU listed as at
least one of the applicants in any IP filing.
Page 16 of 19
d) Due Diligence
Prior to entering any collaboration, the Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell in coordination
with the Research and Development Center (RDC) shall ensure that all agreements comply
with national IP laws and TMU's institutional interests.
e) Approval
All MoUs and collaborative agreements involving potential IP generation must be vetted by
the Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) Cell in coordination with the Research and Development
Center (RDC) and approved by the Vice Chancellor or the designated authority.
X. DEFINITIONS AND OWNERSHIP OF DIGITAL AND EMERGING IP
a) Data and Databases
Data generated through research conducted at TMU, including structured and unstructured
datasets, shall be considered intellectual assets of the University if created using TMU
resources or as part of institutional projects. Databases developed or curated using
University infrastructure shall be co-owned by TMU and the contributors, unless otherwise
governed by project-specific agreements.
b) Software
Software developed using TMU resources, funding, or during the course of institutional
assignments shall be owned by TMU. The creators (faculty, researchers, or students) shall
be acknowledged as inventors or developers, and any commercialization shall follow the
standard revenue-sharing model.
c) Digital Platforms and Tools
Web-based platforms, mobile applications, and digital systems created under TMU
initiatives shall be the property of the University. TMU reserves the right to license, modify,
or distribute such tools in accordance with its institutional objectives and agreements.
d) AI-Created Works
Outputs generated using Artificial Intelligence (AI) tools under TMU-supported research,
when directed or substantially influenced by human intervention, shall be subject to the
same ownership rules as conventional creative or inventive works. Where AI autonomously
contributes to content generation, ownership shall rest with the individual or team
responsible for conceptualizing and deploying the AI model.
XI. RENEWAL OF INTELLECTUAL PROPERTY RIGHTS
The decision to renew any form of Intellectual Property (IP) registered under the name of
Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) shall rest with the University’s IPR Cell, in
coordination with the Research Development Center (RDC). The evaluation for renewal will
Page 17 of 19
be based on the relevance, commercial potential, academic or strategic value, and associated
maintenance costs of the IP.
This policy shall also apply to technologies developed through collaboration with external
entities or licensees, as per the terms and conditions defined in the respective Memorandum of
Understanding (MoU) or licensing agreement. The decision to renew such jointly developed
or licensed IP shall consider the mutual obligations, revenue generation potential, and strategic
interests of TMU and its partners. In all cases, the IPR Cell and RDC shall ensure that renewal
decisions are made in a timely and transparent manner, balancing institutional goals with long-
term innovation outcomes.
XII. GRIEV ANCE REDRESSAL MECHANISM
TMU shall establish a structured two-tier system for addressing grievances related to
intellectual property rights filing, ownership, commercialization, and related disputes.
Tier 1: IPR Appeals Committee: The first level of redressal shall be handled by the IPR
Appeals Committee, comprising:
 One senior faculty member with experience in IP law or commercialization (Chair)
 One legal advisor with IP expertise
 One representative from the IPR Cell
 One representative from the Research and Development Center (RDC)
 One external domain expert
The committee shall review grievances submitted by inventors, authors, or collaborators within
30 days of receipt and provide recommendations based on the facts and applicable policies. A
final decision shall be made after holding a detailed meeting with the Heads of the relevant
Departments, the inventors, and all other parties involved in the dispute.
Tier 2: Final Arbitration: If a party is dissatisfied with the decision of the IPR Appeals
Committee, they may escalate the matter in writing to the Vice Chancellor , whose decision
shall be final and binding.
XIII. MONITORING, AUDITS, AND REPORTING
a) Annual IP Audit
The IPR Cell, in coordination with the Research and Development Center (RDC), shall conduct
an annual Intellectual Property (IP) audit to assess the University’s IP portfolio and evaluate
the effectiveness of IP generation, protection, and commercialization activities.
Page 18 of 19
Audit Objectives Shall Include:
 Reviewing the number of IPR intake form , IP filings (patents, copyrights, trademarks,
designs, and others)
 Monitoring the status of filed applications (filed, published, granted, abandoned)
 Evaluating the number of licenses, assignments, or technology transfers
 Recording revenues generated through IP commercialization
 Identifying startups incubated based on University-owned IP
 Assessing compliance with timelines, agreements, and obligations
b) Reporting
The findings of the annual IP audit shall be compiled into a report and presented to the
Committee for Intellectual Property Facilitation and Advisory. A summarized version may be
included in the University’s annual R&D/Innovation report or strategic plan to track progress
and guide decision-making.
XIV. IP AW ARENESS AND CAPACITY BUILDING
To promote a culture of innovation and informed IP practices, the RDC and IPR Cell shall
organize regular outreach and educational initiatives, including:
 Mandatory annual IPR training sessions for faculty, research staff, and students
 IP bootcamps focused on innovation disclosure, patent searching, drafting, and filing
procedures
 IP Awareness Week every academic year to promote intellectual property literacy,
showcase University IP achievements, and encourage participation in innovation and IP
protection
These activities will be planned in coordination with academic departments and included in the
University's academic calendar and innovation promotion strategy.
XV. RECOGNITION AND INCENTIVE FOR IP FILINGS
To promote a culture of innovation and active participation in intellectual property generation,
Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) shall recognize and reward faculty members,
researchers, staffs and students who demonstrate outstanding contributions in IP creation. The
following measures shall be implemented:
 Annual awards may be given to individuals or departments/colleges filing the highest
number of IP creations.
 Additional recognition may be extended for IP that is granted, licensed, or
commercialized.

settings.
Welcome to the College of Pharmacy at Teerthanker Mahaveer
University (TMU), where academic excellence meets practical experience to shape the
future leaders of the pharmaceutical world. Recognised as one of the top colleges for
pharmacy courses in India, the College provides a unique blend of theoretical knowledge
and hands-on training, preparing our students for successful careers in the dynamic
field of pharmacy.
Read More
Key Highlights
Placement/Internship
Curriculum
Infrastructure
Key Highlights
It is a hub for academic research and consultancy, fostering expertise
by offering a wide range of programmes that equip students with the knowledge and skills
needed to excel in the ever-evolving field of pharmacy.
By offering the PharmD programme, the College of Pharmacy demonstrates
its dedication to elevate the standards of pharmacy education and prepare
graduates/post-graduates for advanced roles in the healthcare system as it is only
available in six pharmacy institutions in Uttar Pradesh.
The college takes pride in its fully functional Drug Information Centre,
which is one of its kind in the state of Uttar Pradesh. This centre serves as a valuable
resource and offers an ideal platform for students to enhance their knowledge in
pharmacovigilance and develop skills in drug information retrieval and analysis.
Placement/Internship
The college has a proven track record of successful placements. Our
graduates have secured positions in diverse roles and settings, including community pharmacies,
hospital pharmacies, pharmaceutical companies, research institutions, regulatory agencies, and
academia.
Center for Teaching, Learning & Development (CTLD) provides comprehensive career guidance and
counselling services to our students in the field of innovation and entrepreneurship.
Teerthanker Mahaveer University's Corporate Resource Center is committed to empowering students
on their journey from admission to placement, with a strong focus on achieving an 82% placement
rate. With personalised assistance in resume building, interview preparation, and professional
training, our students are well-equipped to showcase their skills and abilities to potential
employers.
Curriculum
Programmes are approved by the Pharmacy Council of India.
A meticulously designed curriculum with deliberations from top
professionals and academic bodies to nurture an industry-ready employable workforce.
Emphasis on independent thinking, creativity, and innovation.
A highly disciplined and motivated environment with an extensive focus
on practical based learning of our students.
Infrastructure
The College of Pharmacy has well-equipped laboratories for
Pharmaceutics, Microbiology, Pharmacology, Analysis and Pharmaceutical Chemistry as well
as Pharmacognosy and Herbal Medicine with access to sophisticated instruments.
The major equipment available in this college are IR Spectrophotometer,
HPLC, Lyophilizer, Rotary tablet punch, Digital Dissolution apparatus, Brookfield
Rheometer, Nephelometer and Plethysmometer.
The smart classrooms with advanced technology and cutting-edge
facilities enhance the learning experience, enabling instructors to deliver engaging
presentations and interactive sessions.
The college library is a treasure house of pharmaceutical literature and
resources. Students have access to an extensive collection of books, journals, research
papers, and e-resources, enabling them to stay updated with the latest advancements in
the field.

Why choose
Teerthanker Mahaveer College of
Pharmacy
Key Highlights of College of Pharmacy
+
🏆 UGC-Approved with NAAC A Accreditation: Our programs are recognized by the University Grants
Commission (UGC) and accredited by the National Assessment and Accreditation Council (NAAC) with
an A grade, ensuring high academic standards.
👩‍⚕️ Experiential Learning by Highly Qualified Faculties: Students learn from experienced
professionals who bring real-world insights into the classroom.
✨ 1st Private University in UP with a Drug Information Centre: Gain access to the only Drug
Information Centre in Uttar Pradesh, providing essential drug-related information to healthcare
professionals and consumers.
🏥 Hands-on Training in TMU Hospital: Benefit from practical training in our Hospital with
advanced medical facilities, bridging the gap between theory and practice.
🔬 Central Instrumentation Lab (CIL): Utilize advanced equipment and technologies for
cutting-edge research and practical training.
💉 Well-equipped Laboratories and Medical Technology for Practical Training: Engage in hands-on
learning with our sophisticated lab facilities.
✈️ Global Opportunities: Explore international exposure and collaborations with leading global
pharmaceutical companies.
💼 100% Placement Assistance with an Excellent Track Record: Secure your future with our robust
placement support and strong industry connections.
🌐 Strong Industry Connections for Real-World Insights: Benefit from our partnerships with top
pharmaceutical companies and organizations.
🏫 Modern Classrooms for Interactive Sessions: Learn in an environment designed for interactive
and effective teaching.
🎤 Pharmaceutical Workshops and Seminars: Participate in events that enhance your knowledge and
skills.
🏋️‍♂️ Gym and Fitness Centre: Maintain a healthy lifestyle with our on-campus fitness
facilities.
🏠 Comfortable Hostel Facilities: Enjoy a supportive and comfortable living environment.
🛍️ Shopping Stores and Cafeterias: Access essential amenities right on campus.
🚫 Anti-Ragging Campus: Study in a safe and respectful environment.
📶 Wi-Fi Enabled Campus: Stay connected with high-speed internet access.
Explore Top-Notch Placement Opportunities
+
Our College of Pharmacy is not only a place of learning but also a gateway to
numerous career opportunities. We offer comprehensive placement assistance to help our graduates
secure positions in top pharmaceutical companies.

The Best Private Pharmacy
College in UP
Established in 2008, the College of Pharmacy at TMU has consistently been at
the forefront of pharmaceutical education, research, and practice. Our programs are designed to
equip students with innovative techniques and skills to tackle challenges in drug industries and
clinical areas
Career Opportunities
+
A career in pharmacy offers numerous benefits, including:
Diverse Job Roles:
Pharmacists can work in various settings, including
hospitals, community pharmacies, research labs, and pharmaceutical companies.
High Demand:
With an increasing focus on healthcare, the demand for
qualified pharmacists is on the rise.
Competitive Salaries:
Pharmacists often enjoy lucrative salaries and
benefits.
Global Opportunities:
Pharmaceutical professionals have the chance to
work globally, contributing to healthcare systems worldwide.
Innovative Field:
Pharmacy is a field that constantly evolves with new
technologies and research.
Patient Care:
Pharmacists play a crucial role in patient care, ensuring
safe and effective medication use.
Research Opportunities:
Engage in groundbreaking research that can lead
to new drug discoveries.
Entrepreneurial Ventures:
Pharmacists can start their own pharmacies or
pharmaceutical businesses.
Regulatory Roles:
Work with regulatory agencies to ensure the safety and
efficacy of medications.
Academic Careers:
Teach and mentor the next generation of pharmacy
professionals.
Life at the College of Pharmacy
+
Life at TMU College of Pharmacy is vibrant and enriching. Our students enjoy:
State-of-the-
Art Facilities:
From modern classrooms to well-equipped labs, we provide an optimal learning
environment.
Extracurricular
Activities:
Participate in various clubs, events, and seminars that enhance your
educational experience.
Supportive
Community:
Be part of a diverse and inclusive community that supports your academic and
personal growth.
Conclusion
+
Join the College of Pharmacy at Teerthanker Mahaveer University (TMU) and
embark on a transformative educational journey. Recognized among the top pharmacy colleges in India,
TMU combines academic excellence, practical training, and industry exposure to prepare students for
a successful and rewarding career in the dynamic field of pharmacy.
FAQs
+
Q: What is the duration of the B Pharm course?
Ans:
The B Pharm course duration is four years.
Q: What are the career opportunities after completing a B
Pharm?
Ans:
Graduates can pursue careers in community pharmacies,
hospitals, pharmaceutical companies, research labs, and regulatory agencies.
Q: Does TMU provide placement assistance?
Ans:
Yes, TMU offers 100% placement assistance with an
excellent track record.
Q: What makes TMU College of Pharmacy unique?
Ans:
Our NAAC A accreditation, state-of-the-art facilities,
experiential learning approach, and strong industry connections set us apart.
Q: What specialisations are offered in the M Pharm program?
Ans:
We offer specialisations in Pharmaceutics and
Pharmacology.
Q: Are there global opportunities for TMU pharmacy graduates?
Ans:
Yes, TMU has established strong global networks, offering
students opportunities for international exposure and collaborative research.
Q: What facilities are available on campus?
Ans:
Our campus features modern classrooms, well-equipped
labs, a hospital for practical training, a gym, hostels, shopping stores, cafeterias, and a
Wi-Fi-enabled environment.
Q: How does TMU support research in pharmacy?
Ans:
We provide advanced laboratories, sophisticated
instruments, and a Drug Information Centre to support research and practical training.
Q: What is the role of the Drug Information Centre at TMU?
Ans:
The Drug Information Centre provides essential
drug-related information to healthcare professionals and consumers, supporting medication safety and
efficacy.
Q: How can I apply to TMU College of Pharmacy?
Ans:
You can apply through our website or contact our
admissions office for more details on the application process.

Why TMU is Among the
Top Private Universities in India?
130+ Diverse Academic Programmes
TMU offers
135+ career-oriented programmes
across
14 on-campus colleges and independent departments
, covering
Medical
,

,

,

,

,

,

,

,

,

,

,
Fine Arts
, and
Agriculture
to prepare students for successful careers.
Advanced Research Facilities
The university provides advanced research facilities, access to leading academic resources, and opportunities to collaborate with renowned institutions such as
IBM, Apple, Apollo Hospitals, Hawk Eye Forensic, IEEE, Google, Harvard Business Publishing Education, Lenskart, Aivancity School for Technology, 3SE Experience, Consortium of NLUs, American Heart Association, ALTTC, BSNL, IBM SkillsBuild, IBM Business Analytics Lab, Monash University
and many more.
Students and faculty members are encouraged to engage in impactful research, publish scholarly work, and contribute to innovations that address real-world challenges.
World-Class Infrastructure
Spread across 130+ acres, TMU is equipped with
smart classrooms, cutting-edge laboratories, digital libraries, innovation and research centres, expansive auditoriums, well-furnished hostels, a gymnasium, and extensive sports arenas, including indoor and outdoor stadiums.
TMU's 14 on-campus colleges offer a broad spectrum of academic programs, providing a comprehensive environment where students can thrive in all aspects of their lives.
The university also has a
1000+ bed
multi-speciality hospital
,
offering a wide range of services. It provides comprehensive primary, secondary, and tertiary care, serving as a crucial healthcare resource for the community. The hospital also offers hands-on practical training for medical, nursing, and allied health students, allowing them to gain real-world experience in a professional environment.
Career & Placement Support
The university provides placement assistance through its dedicated Career Guidance and Placement Cell. TMU’s
Corporate Resource Centre
(CRC)
supports students in building successful careers by providing
placement opportunities, internships, industry interactions, recruitment drives, career guidance, and employer connect
. CRC works closely with leading organisations to help students explore suitable career opportunities and transition smoothly from campus to the workplace.
The
Centre for Teaching, Learning & Development
(CTLD)
focuses on developing students’
employability and professional skills
. Through
aptitude training, communication and soft-skills development, personality development, leadership training, group discussions, and interview preparation
, CTLD helps students become confident, industry-ready professionals.
Industry Connections
TMU has over 100 collaborations with leading national and international organisations, research institutions, and corporate partners to provide students with valuable exposure through internships, live projects, industrial visits, expert lectures, workshops, and placement opportunities.
Notable Alumni Network
With over 32,000 alumni, TMU has built a strong network of graduates contributing across diverse fields in India and abroad. Alumni contribute to industry, research, entrepreneurship, and public service while maintaining connections with the university through mentorship, guest lectures, networking events, and career guidance initiatives for current students.
Accreditation & Rankings
NAAC:
Accredited University (A-Grade with CGPA 3.15)
ICAR Accredited:
B.Sc. (Hons.) Agriculture Programme
NBA Accredited:
B.Tech. (Computer Science & Engineering) Programme
NABH Accredited:
TMU Hospital, based on quality of care, state-of-the-art infrastructure, and patient safety
NABL Accredited:
TMU Hospital Medical Laboratories, based on quality and competence of medical laboratories
